\mathrm{gl}^{*}c_{g_{1}+g_{2},n_{1}+n_{2}+1}(e^{\alpha_{0}}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}e_{\alpha_{i}})=c_{g_{1},n_{1}+2}(e^{\alpha_{0}}\otimes\otimes_{i\in I}e_{\alpha_{i}}\otimes e_{\mu})\otimes c_{g_{2},n_{2}+1}(e^{\mu}\otimes\otimes_{j\in J}e_{\alpha_{j}})
for
1\leq\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n_{1}+n_{2}}\leq\dim V
, where
I\sqcup J=\llbracket n_{1}+n_{2}+1\rrbracket\backslash\{1\}
,
|I|=n_{1}
,
|J|=n_{2}
, and
\mathrm{gl}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1},n_{1}+2}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{2},n_{2}+1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1}+g_{2},n_{1}+n_{2}+1}
is the corresponding gluing map.

The gluing map
\mathrm{gl}
creates a nodal curve sewing the last marked point on a curve in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1},n_{1}+2}
(the point labeled by
n_{1}+2
) and the first marked point on a curve in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{2},n_{2}+1}
(the point labeled by
1
) into a node. Under the gluing map
\mathrm{gl}
, the first marked point of a curve in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1},n_{1}+2}
becomes the first marked point on the resulting nodal curve in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1}+g_{2},n_{1}+n_{2}+1}
, and the other marked points are relabeled according to the identification
I\sqcup J=\llbracket n_{1}+n_{2}+1\rrbracket\backslash\{1\}
.

It is easy to see that the validity of the above properties does not depend on the choice of a basis of
V
.

Definition 4.2
.

[
LRZ15
]

A
partial CohFT
is a system of linear maps

c_{g,n}\colon V^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow H^{\mathrm{even}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}),\quad 2g-2+n>0,

where
V
is an arbitrary finite-dimensional vector space, together with a special element
e\in V
, called the
unit
, and a symmetric nondegenerate bilinear form
\eta\in(V^{*})^{\otimes 2}
, called the
metric
, such that, choosing a basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{\dim V}
of
V
, the following axioms are satisfied:

(i)

The maps
c_{g,n}
are equivariant with respect to the
S_{n}
-action permuting the
n
copies of
V
in
V^{\otimes n}
and the
n
marked points in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}
, respectively.

(ii)

We have
\pi^{*}c_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})=c_{g,n+1}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}\otimes e)
for
1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq\dim V
, where
\pi\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}
is the map that forgets the last marked point. Moreover,
c_{0,3}(e_{\alpha}\otimes e_{\beta}\otimes e)=\eta(e_{\alpha}\otimes e_{\beta})=:\eta_{\alpha\beta}
for
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq\dim V
.

(iii)

We have
\mathrm{gl}^{*}c_{g_{1}+g_{2},n_{1}+n_{2}}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}e_{\alpha_{i}})=\eta^{\mu\nu}c_{g_{1},n_{1}+1}(\otimes_{i\in I}e_{\alpha_{i}}\otimes e_{\mu})\otimes c_{g_{2},n_{2}+1}(\otimes_{j\in J}e_{\alpha_{j}}\otimes e_{\nu})
for
1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n_{1}+n_{2}}\leq\dim V
, where
I\sqcup J=\llbracket n_{1}+n_{2}\rrbracket
,
|I|=n_{1}
,
|J|=n_{2}
, and
\mathrm{gl}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1},n_{1}+1}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{2},n_{2}+1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1}+g_{2},n_{1}+n_{2}}
is the corresponding gluing map and where
\eta^{\alpha\beta}
is defined by
\eta^{\alpha\mu}\eta_{\mu\beta}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}
for
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq\dim V
.

Clearly, given a partial CohFT
\{c_{g,n}\colon V^{\otimes n}\to H^{\mathrm{even}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n})\}
, the maps
\widetilde{c}_{g,n+1}\colon V^{*}\otimes V^{\otimes n}\to H^{\mathrm{even}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1})
defined by
\widetilde{c}_{g,n+1}(e^{\alpha_{0}}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}):=\eta^{\alpha_{0}\mu}c_{g,n+1}(e_{\mu}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})
form an F-CohFT.

4.2.2. Various potentials associated to partial CohFTs and F-CohFTs

First consider an arbitrary partial CohFT
\{c_{g,n}\colon V^{\otimes n}\to H^{\mathrm{even}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n})\}
with
\dim V=N
, metric
\eta\colon V^{\otimes 2}\to\mathbb C
, and unit
e\in V
. We fix a basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{N}\in V
and define the
potential
of our partial CohFT by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}:=\sumop\displaylimits\frac{\varepsilon^{2g}}{n!}\left(\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}}c_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}^{d_{i}}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}t^{\alpha_{i}}_{d_{i}}\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon]].

The potential satisfies the
string
and the
dilaton
equations:

(4.1)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}t^{\alpha}_{n+1}\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\alpha\beta}t^{\alpha}_{0}t^{\beta}_{0}+\varepsilon^{2}\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,1}}c_{1,1}(e),

(4.2)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{1}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}t^{\alpha}_{n}\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{n}}+\varepsilon\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}}{{\partial}\varepsilon}-2\mathcal{F}+\varepsilon^{2}\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,1}}\psi_{1}c_{1,1}(e),

where
\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}:=A^{\mu}\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{\mu}_{0}}
and the coefficients
A^{\mu}
are given by
e=A^{\mu}e_{\mu}
. Let us also define formal power series
w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}:=\eta^{\alpha\mu}\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}}{{\partial}t^{\mu}_{0}{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}
and
w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}_{n}:=\frac{{\partial}^{n}w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}}{({\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0})^{n}}
.

For
d\geq 0
, denote by
\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]^{(d)}
the subset of
\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]
formed by infinite linear combinations of monomials
\prodop\displaylimits t^{\alpha_{i}}_{d_{i}}
with
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq d
. Clearly,
\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]^{(d)}\subset\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]
is an ideal. From the string equation (
4.1
), it follows that

(4.3)

\displaystyle w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}_{n}=t^{\alpha}_{n}+\delta_{n,1}A^{\alpha}+R^{\alpha}_{n}(t^{*}_{*})+O(\varepsilon^{2})\quad\text{for some $R^{\alpha}_{n}\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]^{(n+1)}$}.

The following obvious statement will be very useful, so we would like to present it as a separate lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Suppose that a family of formal power series
\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}_{n}\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon]]
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
,
n\geq 0
, satisfies the property
\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}_{n}=t^{\alpha}_{n}+\delta_{n,1}A^{\alpha}+R^{\alpha}_{n}(t^{*}_{*})+O(\varepsilon^{2})
for some
R^{\alpha}_{n}\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]^{(n+1)}
. Then any formal power series in the variables
t^{\alpha}_{a}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
,
a\geq 0
, and
\varepsilon
can be expressed as a formal power series in
\left(\widetilde{w}^{\beta}_{b}-\delta_{b,1}A^{\beta}\right)
and
\varepsilon
in a unique way. In particular, for any two differential polynomials
P,Q\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{w}
, the equality
P|_{w^{\alpha}_{a}=\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}_{a}}=Q|_{w^{\alpha}_{a}=\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}_{a}}
implies that
P=Q
.

In
[
BDGR18
, Proposition 7.2]
, the authors proved that there exists a unique differential polynomial
\mathcal{P}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{w;-2}
such that the difference

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}:=\mathcal{F}-\left.\mathcal{P}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}

satisfies the condition

\mathrm{Coef}_{\varepsilon^{2g}}\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=0\quad\text{if }\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-2

(formally, this was proved for CohFTs, but the proof works for partial CohFTs as well). The formal power series
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}
is called the
reduced potential
of the partial CohFT. Consider the expansions
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 0}\varepsilon^{2g}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}_{g}
and
\mathcal{P}=\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 1}\varepsilon^{2g}\mathcal{P}_{g}
. Note that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}_{0}=\mathcal{F}_{0}
. In
[
BGR19
, Proposition 3.5]
, the authors proved that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}_{g}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}}B^{0}_{g,\overline{d}}c_{g,n}\left(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}\right),\quad\begin{minipage}[199.16928pt]$n\geq 1,\,d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0,\,\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g-1$,\\
$1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq N$.
\end{minipage}

Also note that
\left.\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}_{g}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=0
.

We can now present an explicit formula for the differential polynomial
\mathcal{P}
.

Theorem 4.4
.

For
g\geq 1
, we have
\left.\mathcal{P}_{g}\right|_{w^{*}_{*}=0}=0
and

(4.5)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{P}_{g}}{{\partial}w^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}w^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{w^{*}_{*}=0}=\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}}B^{0}_{g,\overline{d}}c_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}),\quad\begin{minipage}[170.71652pt]$n\geq 1$,\\
$d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0,\,\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}=2g-2$,\\
$1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq N$.
\end{minipage}

Proof.

Let us first recall the construction of the differential polynomial
\mathcal{P}
, following
[
BGR19
, proof of Proposition 3.5]
.

The reduced potential
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}
is constructed by a recursive procedure that kills all of the monomials
\varepsilon^{2g}\prodop\displaylimits t^{\alpha_{i}}_{d_{i}}
with
g\geq 1
and
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-2
in the potential
\mathcal{F}
. Let us assign to such a monomial a level
l:=(g-1)^{2}+\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}
. We see that
(g-1)^{2}\leq l<g^{2}
, which implies that
g
is uniquely determined by
l
. Denote this
g
by
g(l)
. Let us define a sequence of formal power series
\mathcal{F}^{(-1)}:=\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{(0)},\mathcal{F}^{(1)},\ldots
by

(4.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(l)}:=\mathcal{F}^{(l-1)}-\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}\varepsilon^{2g(l)}\hskip-5.69046pt\underline{\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0\\
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}=l-(g(l)-1)^{2}\end{subarray}}\hskip-2.84544pt\left(\mathrm{Coef}_{\varepsilon^{2g(l)}}\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{(l-1)}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}\right)\frac{\prodop\displaylimits(w^{\alpha_{i}}_{d_{i}}-\delta_{d_{i},1}A^{\alpha_{i}})}{n!}},\quad l\geq 0.

From (
4.3
) it follows that going from
\mathcal{F}^{(l-1)}
to
\mathcal{F}^{(l)}
, we kill all of the monomials of level
l
and do not touch the monomials of level strictly less than
l
. Then
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}
is equal to the limit
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}:=\lim_{l\to\infty}\mathcal{F}^{(l)}
, and the underlined terms (multiplied by
\varepsilon^{2g(l)}
), for all
l\geq 0
, produce the required differential polynomial
\mathcal{P}
.

In order to show that
\mathcal{P}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{w;-2}
, we have to check that
\mathcal{P}_{g}^{[k]}=0
for
g\geq 2
and
k<2g-2
. Suppose that this is not true, and denote by
g_{0}
the minimal
g
such that
\mathcal{P}^{[k]}_{g}\neq 0
for some
k<2g-2
. Consider the operator

(4.7)

\displaystyle L:=\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{1}}-\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}t^{\alpha}_{n}\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{n}}-\varepsilon\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}\varepsilon}.

The dilaton equation (
4.2
) implies that
Lw^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}_{n}=nw^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}_{n}
. Therefore, we have the following sequence of equalities:

\varepsilon^{2}\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,1}}\psi_{1}c_{1,1}(e)=(L+2)\mathcal{F}=(L+2)\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}+\underline{\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 2}\varepsilon^{2g}\sumop\displaylimits_{k<2g-2}(k+2-2g)\left.\mathcal{P}^{[k]}_{g}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{n}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}}.

We see that the coefficient
\varepsilon^{2g_{0}}
in
(L+2)\mathcal{F}
is zero. However, the coefficient of
\varepsilon^{2g_{0}}
in
(L+2)\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}
belongs to
\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]^{(2g_{0}-2)}
, while the coefficient of
\varepsilon^{2g_{0}}
in the underlined sum does not belong to
\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*}]]^{(2g_{0}-2)}
, which follows from (
4.3
). This contradiction proves that
\mathcal{P}_{g}\in\mathcal{A}_{w;2g-2}
, and we also see that
\left.\mathcal{P}_{g}\right|_{w^{*}_{*}=0}=0
.

In order to prove Equation (
4.5
), note that the underlined terms in (
4.6
) give a differential polynomial from
\mathcal{A}_{w;\leq l-(g(l)-1)^{2}}
. Using that
\mathcal{P}_{g}\in\mathcal{A}_{w;2g-2}
, we therefore obtain

\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{P}_{g}}{{\partial}w^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}w^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{w^{*}_{*}=0}=\mathrm{Coef}_{\varepsilon^{2g}}\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{(g^{2}-2)}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0},\quad g,n\geq 1,\,\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}=2g-2.

Following
[
BGR19
, proof of Proposition 3.5]
, we call a tree
T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c)}_{g,n,0;\circ}

(j,d)
-admissible
if for any
1\leq k<\deg(T)
, we have
g_{k}(T)\leq j
and

\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in H^{e}_{+}(T)\\
l(h)=k\end{subarray}}q(h)\leq\begin{cases}2g_{k}(T)-2&\text{if $g_{k}(T)<j$},\\
d&\text{if $g_{k}(T)=j$}.\end{cases}

Denote by
\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c);(j,d)}_{g,n,0;\circ}\subset\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c)}_{g,n,0;\circ}
the set of such trees. By
[
BGR19
, Equation (19)]
, we have

\mathrm{Coef}_{\varepsilon^{2g}}\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{(g^{2}-2)}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c);(g,2g-3)}_{g,n,0;\circ}}(-1)^{\deg(T)-1}\mathrm{e}_{*}[T,\overline{d}]\right)c_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})

for
g,n\geq 1
and
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}=2g-2
, and it remains to check that

\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c);(g,2g-3)}_{g,n,0;\circ}}(-1)^{\deg(T)-1}\mathrm{e}_{*}[T,\overline{d}]=B^{0}_{g,\overline{d}}.

Clearly,
\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c);(g,2g-3)}_{g,n,0;\circ}\subset\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c,a)}_{g,n,0;\circ}
. The converse inclusion is not necessarily true; however,
\left\{\left.T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c,a)}_{g,n,0;\circ}\;\right|\;\mathrm{e}_{*}[T,\overline{d}]\neq 0\right\}\subset\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c);(g,2g-3)}_{g,n,0;\circ}
thanks to the following statement proved in
[
BGR19
]
.

Lemma 4.5

(
cf.

[
BGR19
, Lemma 3.6]
)
.

Let
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
,
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0
, and
T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,c)}_{g,n,0;\circ}
. Suppose
\mathrm{e}_{*}[T,\overline{d}]\neq 0
and
g_{k}(T)=g_{k+1}(T)
for some
1\leq k<\deg(T)
. Then

\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\\
l(h)=k+1\end{subarray}}q(h)>\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\\
l(h)=k\end{subarray}}q(h).

This concludes the proof of Theorem
4.4
.
∎

Now consider an arbitrary F-CohFT
\{c_{g,n+1}\colon V^{*}\otimes V^{\otimes n}\to H^{\mathrm{even}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1})\}
with
\dim V=N
and unit
e\in V
. We fix a basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{N}\in V
and assign to the F-CohFT a collection of potentials
\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
,
a\geq 0
, by setting

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}:=\sumop\displaylimits\frac{\varepsilon^{2g}}{n!}\left(\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1}}c_{g,n+1}(e^{\alpha}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})\psi_{1}^{a}\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i+1}^{d_{i}}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}t^{\alpha_{i}}_{d_{i}}\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon]].

These potentials satisfy the following system of equations, which can be considered as an analog of the string equation (
4.1
):

(4.8)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}=\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a-1}+\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}t^{\beta}_{n+1}\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{n}},\quad 1\leq\alpha\leq N,\,a\geq 0,

where we adopt the convention
\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,-1}:=t^{\alpha}_{0}
. There is also an analog of the dilaton equation:

(4.9)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{1}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}t^{\beta}_{n}\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{n}}+\varepsilon\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}\varepsilon}-\mathcal{F},\quad 1\leq\alpha\leq N,\,a\geq 0.

Similarly to the case of a partial CohFT, let us define
w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}:=\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,0}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}
and
w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}_{n}:=\frac{{\partial}^{n}w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}}{({\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0})^{n}}
. From (
4.8
), it follows that the formal power series
w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}
satisfy the property (
4.3
).

For
m,n\geq 1
, denote by
\mathrm{perm}_{m,n}
the map
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+m}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+m}
induced by the permutation of marked points
(1,\ldots,m,m+1,\ldots,m+n)\mapsto(m+1,\ldots,m+n,1,\ldots,m)
.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let us fix
1\leq\alpha\leq N
,
a\geq 0
,
k\geq 0
, and
k
-tuples
\overline{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k})
,
\overline{b}=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{k})
, where
1\leq\beta_{i}\leq N
,
b_{i}\geq 0
.

(1)

There exists a unique differential polynomial
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{w;k-1}
such that the difference

{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}:=\frac{{\partial}^{k}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta_{1}}_{b_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\beta_{k}}_{b_{k}}}-\left.\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}

satisfies the condition

\mathrm{Coef}_{\varepsilon^{2g}}\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=0\quad\text{if}\quad\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-1+k.

We consider the expansions
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 0}\varepsilon^{2g}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b},g}
and
{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 0}\varepsilon^{2g}{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b},g}
.

(2)

We have
\left.{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b},g}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=0
and

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b},g}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+k+1}}\mathrm{perm}_{k+1,n}^{*}\left(B^{k+1}_{g,\overline{d}}\right)c_{g,n+k+1}\left(e^{\alpha}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{k}e_{\beta_{i}}\otimes\otimes_{j=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{j}}\right)\psi_{1}^{a}\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{k}\psi_{i+1}^{b_{i}},

where
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
,
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0
,
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g+k
, and
1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq N
.

(3)

We have
\left.\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b},g}\right|_{w^{*}_{*}=0}=0
and

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b},g}}{{\partial}w^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}w^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{w^{*}_{*}=0}=\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+k+1}}\mathrm{perm}_{k+1,n}^{*}\left(B^{k+1}_{g,\overline{d}}\right)c_{g,n+k+1}\left(e^{\alpha}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{k}e_{\beta_{i}}\otimes\otimes_{j=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{j}}\right)\psi_{1}^{a}\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{k}\psi_{i+1}^{b_{i}},

where
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
,
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0
,
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}=2g-1+k
, and
1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq N
.

Proof.

(
1
) The result is analogous to
[
BDGR18
, Proposition 7.2]
, whose proof we briefly explained in the proof of Theorem
4.4
. Regarding the existence part, we construct the formal power series
{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}
, together with the differential polynomial
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}
, by a recursive procedure that kills all of the monomials
\varepsilon^{2g}\prodop\displaylimits t^{\alpha_{i}}_{d_{i}}
with
g\geq 0
and
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-1+k
in
\frac{{\partial}^{k}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta_{1}}_{b_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\beta_{k}}_{b_{k}}}
. One then checks that the resulting differential polynomial
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}
has degree
k-1
using the equation
L\frac{{\partial}^{k}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta_{1}}_{b_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\beta_{k}}_{b_{k}}}=(k-1)\frac{{\partial}^{k}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta_{1}}_{b_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\beta_{k}}_{b_{k}}}
(where the operator
L
was defined in (
4.7
)), which is a consequence of Equation (
4.9
), and property (
4.3
), which, as we already remarked, holds for F-CohFTs. The uniqueness part follows again from (
4.3
).

(
2
) The result is analogous to
[
BGR19
, Proposition 3.5]
, and the proof is obtained from the proof of that proposition by easily seen adjustments.

(
3
) This is analogous to Theorem
4.4
.
∎

4.3. The DZ hierarchy for an F-CohFT and Conjecture
1

We consider an arbitrary F-CohFT.

4.3.1. Construction of the DZ hierarchy

Denote by
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{wk}}_{w}
the ring of formal power series in the shifted variables
(w^{\alpha}_{n}-A^{\alpha}\delta_{n,1})
, and let
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{wk}}_{w}:=\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{wk}}_{w}[[\varepsilon]]
. We have the obvious inclusion
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{w}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{wk}}_{w}
. From property (
4.3
), it follows that for any
1\leq\alpha\leq N
,
a\geq 0
, and
k
-tuples
\overline{\beta}\in\llbracket N\rrbracket^{k}
and
\overline{b}\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}^{k}
, there exists a unique element
{}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{wk}}_{w}
such that

\frac{{\partial}^{k}\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,a}}{{\partial}t^{\beta_{1}}_{b_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\beta_{k}}_{b_{k}}}=\left.{}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}.

Clearly, we have
\frac{{\partial}w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}=\left.{\partial}_{x}{}^{\alpha,0}_{\beta,b}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}
, which implies that the
N
-tuple of formal powers series
w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}
satisfies the system of generalized PDEs

(4.10)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}w^{\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}={\partial}_{x}{}^{\alpha,0}_{\beta,b},\quad 1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq N,\,b\geq 0,

which we call the
Dubrovin–Zhang
(
DZ
)
hierarchy
associated to our F-CohFT. We say “generalized PDEs” because the right-hand sides are not differential polynomials but elements of the larger ring
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{wk}}_{w}
. The
N
-tuple
\overline{w}^{\,\mathrm{top}}:=(w^{\mathrm{top};1},\ldots,w^{\mathrm{top};N})
is clearly a solution of the DZ hierarchy, which is called the
topological solution
.

4.3.2. Conjecture
1
and the polynomiality of the DZ hierarchy

From Theorem
4.6
, it follows that the validity of Conjecture
1
for some fixed
m\geq 2
implies that
{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}=0
if
l(\overline{\beta})=l(\overline{b})=m-1
, or equivalently
{}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}=\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,a}_{\overline{\beta},\overline{b}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{w;m-2}
. In particular, we obtain the following result.

Theorem 4.7
.

The validity of Conjecture
1
for
m=2
implies that the right-hand sides of the equations of the DZ hierarchy (
4.10
) associated to an arbitrary F-CohFT are differential polynomials of degree
1
. Moreover, part (
3
) of Theorem
4.6
then gives a geometric formula for these differential polynomials.

Also note that, assuming the validity of Conjecture
1
for
m=2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{\gamma}_{c}}\frac{{\partial}w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}=\left.\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}\frac{{\partial}\left({\partial}_{x}{}^{\alpha,0}_{\beta,b}\right)}{{\partial}w^{\mu}_{n}}{\partial}_{x}^{n+1}{}^{\mu,0}_{\gamma,c}\right|_{w^{\delta}_{d}=w^{\mathrm{top};\delta}_{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\mathrel{\rotatebox{90.0}{$=$}}

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}\frac{{\partial}w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\gamma}_{c}}=\left.\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}\frac{{\partial}\left({\partial}_{x}{}^{\alpha,0}_{\gamma,c}\right)}{{\partial}w^{\mu}_{n}}{\partial}_{x}^{n+1}{}^{\mu,0}_{\beta,b}\right|_{w^{\delta}_{d}=w^{\mathrm{top};\delta}_{d}},

which, by Lemma
4.3
, implies that the flows of the DZ hierarchy commute pairwise.

4.4. The DR hierarchy for an F-CohFT and Conjectures
2
and
3

4.4.1. The definition of the DR hierarchy

Consider an arbitrary F-CohFT of rank
N
. Let
u^{1},\ldots,u^{N}
be formal variables, and consider the associated ring of differential polynomials
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{u}
. Define differential polynomials
P^{\alpha}_{\beta,d}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{u;0}
,
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq N
,
d\geq 0
, by

\displaystyle P^{\alpha}_{\beta,d}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g,n\geq 0,\,2g+n>0\\
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\geq 0\\
\sumop\displaylimits_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=2g\end{subarray}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2g}}{n!}\prodop\displaylimits_{j=1}^{n}u^{\alpha_{j}}_{k_{j}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\sumop\displaylimits_{g,n\geq 0},}\times\mathrm{Coef}_{(a_{1})^{k_{1}}\cdots(a_{n})^{k_{n}}}\left(\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+2}}\hskip-14.22636pt\lambda_{g}{\mathrm{DR}_{g}\bigg(-\sumop\displaylimits_{j=1}^{n}a_{j},0,a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\bigg)}\psi_{2}^{d}c_{g,n+2}(e^{\alpha}\otimes e_{\beta}\otimes\otimes_{j=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{j}})\right).

The
DR hierarchy
, see
[
BR21
]
, is the following system of evolutionary PDEs:

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}u^{\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{d}}={\partial}_{x}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,d},\quad 1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq N,\;d\geq 0.

In
[
BR21
, Theorem 5.1]
, the authors proved that all of the equations of the DR hierarchy are compatible with each other.

In
[
ABLR21
, Theorem 1.5]
, the authors proved that

(4.11)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,d}}{{\partial}u^{{1\!\!1}}}=\begin{cases}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,d-1}&\text{if $d\geq 1$},\\
\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&\text{if $d=0$}.\end{cases}

Lemma 4.8
.

The DR hierarchy satisfies the following properties.

(1)

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,0}}{{\partial}u^{{1\!\!1}}_{x}}=0
.

(2)

The DR hierarchy has a unique solution
\overline{u}^{\mathrm{str}}=(u^{\mathrm{str};1},\ldots,u^{\mathrm{str};N})
satisfying the condition
\left.u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}\right|_{t^{*}_{\geq 1}=0}=t^{\alpha}_{0}
, where we identify the derivatives
{\partial}_{x}
and
\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}
.

Proof.

(
1
) This follows immediately from (
2.2
) and the definition of
P^{\alpha}_{\beta,0}
.

(
2
) Using Equation (
4.11
) and part (
1
), we compute

\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}u^{1\!\!1}_{x}}{\partial}_{x}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,0}={\partial}_{x}\frac{{\partial}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,0}}{{\partial}u^{1\!\!1}_{x}}+\frac{{\partial}P^{\alpha}_{\beta,0}}{{\partial}u^{1\!\!1}}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta},

which completes the proof.
∎

4.4.2. A collection of potentials associated to the DR hierarchy

Define
u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}_{n}:=\frac{{\partial}^{n}u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}}{({\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0})^{n}}
. Let us introduce
N
formal power series
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon]]
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
, by the relation

\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}:=\left.P^{\alpha}_{\beta,b}\right|_{u^{\gamma}_{n}=u^{\mathrm{str};\gamma}_{n}},

with the constant terms defined to be equal to zero,
\left.\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}:=0
. Consider the expansion
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}=\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 0}\varepsilon^{2g}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}_{g}
.

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
,
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0
, and
1\leq\alpha,\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq N
. Then we have

\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}_{g}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if\, $\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-1$},\\
\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1}}\mathrm{perm}_{1,n}^{*}\left(A^{1}_{g,\overline{d}}\right)c_{g,n+1}\left(e^{\alpha}\otimes\otimes_{j=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{j}}\right)&\text{if\, $\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g$}.\end{cases}

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the proof of analogous statements in the case of the DR hierarchy associated to a CohFT (see
[
BDGR18
, Proposition 6.10]
and
[
BDGR20
, Theorem 6.1]
). So we only very briefly sketch the details.

Consider a stable tree
T\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,1}
. We will call a level function
l\colon V(T)\to\mathbb Z_{\geq 1}

injective
if
|l^{-1}(i)|=1
for each
1\leq i\leq\deg(l)
. Clearly, such a function gives a bijection between the sets
V(T)
and
\llbracket|V(T)|\rrbracket
. Denote by
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}(T)
the set of all injective level functions on
T
. For
l\in\mathcal{L}^{(i)}(T)
, denote by
\overline{(T,l)}
the stable tree from the set
\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n+|V(T)|,1}
obtained as follows:

•

We attach to each vertex
v
of
T
a new regular leg and label it by the number
l(v)+n
.

•

We relabel the unique frozen leg of
T
by the number
n+|V(T)|+1
.

For an
N
-tuple
{\overline{Q}}=(Q^{1},\ldots,Q^{N})\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{u}^{N}
, denote by
D_{{\overline{Q}}}
the linear operator in
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{u}
defined by

D_{{\overline{Q}}}:=\sumop\displaylimits_{k\geq 0}\left({\partial}_{x}^{k}Q^{\gamma}\right)\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}u^{\gamma}_{k}}.

Let
{\overline{P}}_{\beta,d}:=(P^{1}_{\beta,d},\ldots,P^{N}_{\beta,d})
. From the definition of the formal power series
u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}
, it follows that

\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}_{g}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=\mathrm{Coef}_{\varepsilon^{2g}}\left.\left(D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}_{\alpha_{n},d_{n}}}\cdots D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}_{\alpha_{3},d_{3}}}D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}_{\alpha_{2},d_{2}}}P^{\alpha}_{\alpha_{1},d_{1}}\right)\right|_{u^{\gamma}_{k}=\delta_{k,1}A^{\gamma}}.

In the same way as in the proof of
[
BDGR18
, Lemma 6.9]
, this formula implies that

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}_{g}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{(2g+n-1)!}\mathrm{Coef}_{a_{1}\cdots a_{2g+n-1}}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{n}_{g,2g+n-1,1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{l\in\mathcal{L}^{(i)}(T)}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\hphantom{=}\left.\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2g+2n}}\hskip-14.22636pt\lambda_{g}\,\mathrm{perm}^{*}_{1,2g+2n-1}\mathrm{DR}_{\overline{(T,l)}}\left(A,\overline{0}_{n},-\sumop\displaylimits a_{i}\right)\,c_{g,2g+2n}\left(e^{\alpha}\otimes e^{\otimes(2g+n-1)}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{2g+n+i}^{d_{i}}\right),

where
A=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{2g+n-1})
and
\overline{0}_{n}:=(0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb Z^{n}
. In the case
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-1
, the proof of the vanishing of the right-hand side of this equation goes along the same lines as the proof of
[
BDGR18
, Lemma 6.11]
. Indeed,
[
BDGR18
, Lemma 6.11]
says that the right-hand side of the equation vanishes if we replace the class
c_{g,2g+2n}(e^{\alpha}\otimes e^{\otimes(2g+n-1)}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})
with the class
c_{g,2g+2n}(e\otimes e^{\otimes(2g+n-1)}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})
with
c_{g,2g+2n}
being a CohFT. However, one can easily see that the same proof works in our case as well.

We now consider the case
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g
. For a tree
\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,1}
and
v\in V(T)
, define

\mathrm{ind}(v):=\min\{1\leq i\leq n\;|\;\text{$\sigma_{i}$ is attached to $v$}\}.

If
v
is not incident to any regular leg, then we write
\mathrm{ind}(v):=\infty
. Let us call the tree
T

special
(in
[
BDGR20
, Section 6.5.1]
, the authors said “admissible”) if

a)

\mathrm{ind}(v)<\infty
for any vertex
v\in V(T)
;

b)

\mathrm{ind}(v_{1})<\mathrm{ind}(v_{2})
for any two distinct vertices
v_{1},v_{2}\in V(T)
such that
v_{2}
is a descendant of
v_{1}
.

Denote by
\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,1}^{(s)}\subset\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,1}
the subset of all special trees.

For a tree
T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(s)}_{g,n,1}
, define

S_{T}:=\left\{\overline{c}=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}^{n}\;\left|\;\begin{minipage}[128.0374pt]$c_{i}=0$ unless $i=\mathrm{ind}(v)$ for some $v\in V(T)$
\end{minipage}\right.\right\}.

Let
d\geq 2g
. We claim that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0\\
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}=d\end{subarray}}\frac{\prodop\displaylimits b_{i}^{d_{i}}}{(2g+n-1)!}\mathrm{Coef}_{a_{1}\cdots a_{2g+n-1}}\left(\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{n}_{g,2g+n-1,1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{l\in\mathcal{L}^{(i)}(T)}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2g+2n}}\hskip-14.22636pt\lambda_{g}\,\mathrm{perm}^{*}_{1,2g+2n-1}\mathrm{DR}_{\overline{(T,l)}}\Big(A,\overline{0}_{n},-\sumop\displaylimits a_{i}\Big)\,c_{g,2g+2n}\left(e^{\alpha}\otimes e^{\otimes(2g+n-1)}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{2g+n+i}^{d_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(s)}_{g,n,1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{c}\in S_{T}\\
\sumop\displaylimits c_{i}=d-(2g+|V(T)|-1)\end{subarray}}\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1}}\hskip-14.22636pt\lambda_{g}\,\mathrm{perm}^{*}_{1,n}\mathrm{DR}_{T}\left(B,-\sumop\displaylimits b_{i}\right)c_{g,n+1}(e^{\alpha}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}})\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}(b_{i}\psi_{i+1})^{c_{i}},

where
B=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
. Indeed, in
[
BDGR20
, Section 6.5.2]
, this equation is proved if we replace the classes
c_{g,k+1}(e^{\alpha}\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{k}v_{i})
,
v_{i}\in V
, with the classes
c_{g,k+1}(e\otimes\otimes_{i=1}^{k}v_{i})
with
c_{g,k+1}
being a CohFT. However, one can easily check that the same proof works in our case as well.

Therefore, it is sufficient to check the cohomological relation

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(s)}_{g,n,1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{c}\in S_{T}\\
\sumop\displaylimits c_{i}=d-(2g+|V(T)|-1)\end{subarray}}\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{T}\left(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n},-\sumop\displaylimits b_{i}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}(b_{i}\psi_{i})^{c_{i}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{d-2g+1}_{g,n,1}}C(T)\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{T}\left(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n},-\sumop\displaylimits b_{i}\right),

but this was done in
[
BDGR20
, Equation (6.13)]
.
∎

Note that the definition of formal power series
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}
implies that
\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{d}}\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}=\frac{{\partial}u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{\beta}_{d}}
. Since, by Theorem
4.9
, we have
\left.\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=0
, we conclude that

\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}=u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}.

4.4.3. Conjecture
2
and the equivalence of the two hierarchies for F-CohFTs

Consider an arbitrary F-CohFT. Suppose that Conjecture
2
is true. Then Theorems
4.6
and
4.9
imply that
{}^{\mathrm{red};\alpha,0}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR};\alpha}
and therefore

\mathcal{F}^{\alpha,0}-\left.\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,0}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR},\alpha}.

Differentiating both sides by
\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}
, we obtain

w^{\mathrm{top};\alpha}-\left.\left({\partial}_{x}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,0}\right)\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}=u^{\mathrm{str};\alpha}.

Consider the Miura transformation

(4.12)

\displaystyle w^{\alpha}\longmapsto u^{\alpha}(w^{*}_{*},\varepsilon)=w^{\alpha}-{\partial}_{x}\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,0}

and its inverse
u^{\alpha}\mapsto w^{\alpha}(u^{*}_{*},\varepsilon)
. We see that the Miura transformation (
4.12
) transforms the DR hierarchy to a hierarchy having the
N
-tuple
\overline{w}^{\,\mathrm{top}}
as its solution. Lemma
4.3
implies that this hierarchy coincides with the DZ hierarchy corresponding to our F-CohFT. In particular, we obtain that the right-hand side of each equation of the DZ hierarchy (
4.10
) is a differential polynomial of degree
1
.

Summarizing, we obtain the following result.

Theorem 4.10
.

Conjecture
2
implies that the DZ hierarchy corresponding to an arbitrary F-CohFT is polynomial and that it is related to the DR hierarchy by the Miura transformation (
4.12
). Moreover, there is a geometric formula for the differential polynomials
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\alpha,0}
defining this Miura transformation, given by part (
3
) of Theorem
4.6
.

4.4.4. Conjecture
3
and the DR/DZ equivalence conjecture for partial CohFTs

Following
[
BGR19
]
, let us recall here the relation between Conjecture
3
and the DR/DZ equivalence conjecture for partial CohFTs, proposed in
[
BDGR18
]
. Again, what we will say was discussed in
[
BGR19
]
and
[
BDGR18
]
for CohFTs, but the required results are true for partial CohFTs, with the same proofs.

Consider an arbitrary partial CohFT, the associated F-CohFT, and the corresponding DR hierarchy. Define

h_{\alpha,p}:=\eta_{{1\!\!1}\mu}P^{\mu}_{\alpha,p+1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{u;0},\quad 1\leq\alpha\leq N,\,\,p\geq-1.

In
[
BDGR18
]
, the authors proved that for any
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq N
and
p,q\geq 0
, there exists a unique differential polynomial
{}^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\alpha,p;\beta,q}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{u;0}
such that

{\partial}_{x}{}^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\alpha,p;\beta,q}=D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}_{\beta,q}}h_{\alpha,p-1},\quad\left.{}^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\alpha,p;\beta,q}\right|_{u^{*}_{*}=0}=0.

In
[
BDGR18
]
, the authors proved that there exists a unique formal power series
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}(t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon)=\sumop\displaylimits_{g\geq 0}\varepsilon^{2g}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}_{g}(t^{*}_{*})\in\mathbb C[[t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon]]
such that

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{p}{\partial}t^{\beta}_{q}}=\left.{}^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\alpha,p;\beta,q}\right|_{u^{\gamma}_{c}=u^{\mathrm{str};\gamma}_{c}},

\displaystyle 1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq N,\quad p,q\geq 0,

\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}}{{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}=\sumop\displaylimits_{n\geq 0}t^{\alpha}_{n+1}\frac{{\partial}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\alpha\beta}t^{\alpha}_{0}t^{\beta}_{0},

\displaystyle\left.\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=0.

The formal power series
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}
is called the
potential
of the DR hierarchy. By
[
BDGR18
, Proposition 6.10]
and
[
BDGR20
, Theorem 6.1]
, for
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
,
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0
, and
1\leq\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}\leq N
, we have

(4.13)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{{\partial}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{n}}_{d_{n}}}\right|_{t^{*}_{*}=0}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\leq 2g-2$},\\
\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}}A^{0}_{g,\overline{d}}\,c_{g,n}\left(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}\right)&\text{if $\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g-1$}.\end{cases}

Suppose that Conjecture
3
is true. Then Equations (
4.4
) and (
4.13
) imply that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{red}}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}
or, equivalently,

(4.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}-\left.\mathcal{P}\right|_{w^{\gamma}_{c}=w^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{c}}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}},

which was formulated in
[
BDGR18
]
and called the
strong DR/DZ equivalence conjecture
. Let us discuss consequences of Equation (
4.14
).

Consider the Miura transformation

u^{\alpha}\longmapsto{\widetilde{u}}^{\,\alpha}(u^{*}_{*},\varepsilon):=\eta^{\alpha\mu}{}^{\mathrm{DR}}_{\mu,0;{1\!\!1},0}.

The variables
{\widetilde{u}}^{\alpha}
are called the
normal coordinates
of the DR hierarchy. Obviously, the
N
-tuple of formal power series

{\widetilde{u}}^{\;\mathrm{str};\alpha}(t^{*}_{*},\varepsilon):=\eta^{\alpha\mu}\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{DR}}}{{\partial}t^{\mu}_{0}{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}},\quad 1\leq\alpha\leq N,

is a solution of the DR hierarchy written in the normal coordinates. Now consider the following Miura transformation relating the variables
w^{\alpha}
and
{\widetilde{u}}^{\,\alpha}
:

(4.15)

\displaystyle w^{\alpha}\longmapsto{\widetilde{u}}^{\,\alpha}(w^{*}_{*},\varepsilon):=w^{\alpha}-\eta^{\alpha\mu}{\partial}_{x}D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}_{\mu,0}}\mathcal{P}.

Equation (
4.14
) implies that the DR hierarchy written in the variables
w^{\alpha}
has the
N
-tuple
\overline{w}^{\,\mathrm{top}}
as its solution. Therefore, by Lemma
4.3
, this hierarchy coincides with the DZ hierarchy corresponding to our partial CohFT. So we obtain that the DZ hierarchy corresponding to our partial CohFT is polynomial and that it is related to the DR hierarchy by a Miura transformation.

In fact, as is explained in
[
BDGR18
]
, Equation (
4.14
) implies that there is a relation between the two hierarchies that is stronger than the Miura equivalence. Both hierarchies are Hamiltonian, and they are endowed with a tau-structure, meaning that there a special choice of densities of the Hamiltonians satisfying certain properties. For the DZ hierarchy, these densities are
\eta_{{1\!\!1}\mu}{}^{\mu,0}_{\alpha,p+1}
, and for the DR hierarchy, these densities are
h_{\alpha,p}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq N
,
p\geq 0
. In
[
BDGR18
]
, the authors proved that Equation (
4.14
) implies that the two hierarchies, together with their Hamiltonian structure and tau-structure, are related by the normal Miura transformation given by (
4.15
). Moreover, there is a geometric formula for the differential polynomial
\mathcal{P}
describing this normal Miura transformation, given by Theorem
4.4
.

5. Proof of Theorem
2.2

5.1. Proof of Conjecture
1
for
\boldsymbol{n=1}

In the case
n=1
, Conjecture
1
says that
B^{m}_{g,d}=0
for
m\geq 2
and
d\geq 2g+m-1
. Let us prove that. To explain the proof, we need some notation. We denote by
\Gamma^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}
,
g\geq 0
,
k\geq 1
, the following tautological class:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}\coloneqq\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1},\dots,g_{k}\\
d_{1},\dots,d_{k}\end{subarray}}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&&\cr&&&&&&\cr&&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise R^{d}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,m+1}),

where
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{k}+k-1=d
,
g_{1}+\cdots+g_{k}=g
,
g_{1},\dots,g_{k-1}\geq 1
,

g_{k}\geq 0
if
m\geq 2
and
g_{k}\geq 1
if
m=0,1
,
and for any
i=2,\dots,k
, we have
d_{i}+\cdots+d_{k}+k-i\leq 2(g_{i}+\cdots+g_{k})+m-2
.

Lemma 5.1
.

We have
B^{m}_{g,d}=B^{m}_{g,2g+m-1}\psi_{1}^{d-2g-m+1}
for
d\geq 2g+m-1
and

\displaystyle B^{m}_{g,2g+m-1}=\sumop\displaylimits_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k+1}\Gamma^{g,m}_{2g+m-1\mathop{|}k}.

Proof.

This follows directly from unfolding the definitions. Note that the sum over
k
is in fact finite since
\Gamma^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}=0
for
k\geq g+2
for any
m
and
\Gamma^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}=0
for
k\geq g+1
for
m=0,1
.
∎

By this lemma, it is sufficient to prove that
B^{m}_{g,2g+m-1}=0
,
g\geq 0
,
m\geq 2
. The proof is based on the following Liu–Pandharipande relations, see
[
LP11
, Propositions 1.1 and 1.2]
(see also
[
BHIS22
, Corollary 3.2]
), in the tautological ring:

for any

.

,

, and

, let

be the sum of decorated stable graphs given as

where
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{k}+k-1=d
,
g_{1}+\cdots+g_{k}=g
,

, and for any

, we have
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{i}+i-1\leq 2(g_{1}+\cdots+g_{i})-1
. For
d\geq 2g
, we define
\gamma^{g}_{d\mathop{|}k}\coloneqq 0
. Note that
\gamma^{g}_{d\mathop{|}k}=0
for
k>g
. We also define

\widetilde{\gamma}^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\Gamma^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}&\text{if $d\leq 2g+m-2$},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
\gamma_{1}\diamond\gamma_{2}
be the operation of concatenation of two decorated stable graphs
\gamma_{1}\in G_{g_{1},2}
and
\gamma_{2}\in G_{g_{2},m+1}
that forms an edge from the second leg of
\gamma_{1}
and the first leg of
\gamma_{2}
.

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)\diamond\widetilde{\gamma}^{g_{3},m}_{d_{3}\mathop{|}k_{3}}.

Before proving this lemma, let us show how to derive the desired vanishing
B^{m}_{g,2g+m-1}=0
from it. Indeed, consider Equation (
5.19
) for
\ell=g+1
. Note that since
m\geq 2
, we have
\Gamma^{g,m}_{d\mathop{|}k}=0
for
g+1<k
. Hence the left-hand side of Equation (
5.19
) with
\ell\geq g
, so in particular for
\ell=g+1
, is equal to
B^{m}_{g,2g+m-1}
.

On the right-hand side, we have
\gamma^{g_{1}}_{d_{1}\mathop{|}g+1}=0
in the first term (since
g_{1}\leq g<g+1
) and
\widetilde{\gamma}^{g_{2},m}_{d_{2}\mathop{|}g+1}=0
in the second term (since
g_{2}+1<g+1
). Consider the third term. We have
k_{1}+k_{3}=g+1
and
g_{1}+g_{3}<g
, so either
g_{1}<k_{1}
, or
g_{3}+1<k_{3}
, or both. Hence in the third term either
\gamma^{g_{1}}_{d_{1}\mathop{|}k_{1}}=0
, or
\widetilde{\gamma}^{g_{3},m}_{d_{3}\mathop{|}k_{3}}=0
, or both. Hence the right-hand side of Equation (
5.19
) with
\ell=g+1
is equal to
0
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

We prove the lemma by induction. The base is the
\ell=1
case. It is equivalent to

\displaystyle\Gamma^{g,m}_{2g+m-1\mathop{|}1}=\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1}\geq 1,\,g_{2}\geq 0\\
d_{1}+d_{2}=2g+m-2\\
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}(-1)^{d_{1}}\gamma^{g_{1}}_{d_{1}\mathop{|}1}\diamond\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 21.8073pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-8.93748pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\ellipsed@{7.80728pt}{6.12498pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 17.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{g_{2}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.816pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.40334pt\raise 6.92557pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\,\,\psi^{d_{3}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\diamond\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{g_{3}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.816pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 2.70714pt\raise 6.92557pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{d_{3}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\diamond\widetilde{\gamma}^{g_{4},m}_{d_{4}\mathop{|}k_{4}}.

Note that either
d_{1}+d_{2}\leq 2(g_{1}+g_{2})-2
or
d_{3}+d_{4}\leq 2(g_{3}+g_{4})+m-3
, but not both. Hence (
5.1
) is equal to the sum of the
k_{1}\geq 2
terms in (
5.1
) and the
k_{3}\geq 2
terms in (
5.1
).

Summarizing the computations above, we see that the sum of (
5.1
), (
5.1
), and (
5.1
) is equal to the sum of (
5.1
), (
5.1
), (
5.1
), and (
5.1
). This proves the induction step and completes the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
∎

5.2. Proof of Conjecture
2
for
\boldsymbol{n=1}

We have to check that
B^{1}_{g,d}=A^{1}_{g,d}
for any
g\geq 1
and
d\geq 2g
. From the definition of the class
B^{1}_{g,d}
, it follows that

B^{1}_{g,d}=\psi_{1}^{d-2g}B^{1}_{g,2g}.

On the other hand, setting
k:=d-2g
, we have

A^{1}_{g,d}=\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+\cdots+g_{k}=g\\
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{g_{k-i}}{g_{1}+\cdots+g_{k-i}}\right)\lambda_{g_{1}}\mathrm{DR}_{g_{1}}(1,-1)\diamond\lambda_{g_{2}}\mathrm{DR}_{g_{2}}(1,-1)\diamond\cdots\diamond\lambda_{g_{k}}\mathrm{DR}_{g_{k}}(1,-1).

Using the formula

\psi_{1}\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{g}(1,-1)=\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1},g_{2}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{2}}{g}\lambda_{g_{1}}\mathrm{DR}_{g_{1}}(1,-1)\diamond\lambda_{g_{2}}\mathrm{DR}_{g_{2}}(1,-1),

cf.

[
BSSZ15
, Theorem 4]
, it is easy to check by induction that

A^{1}_{g,d}=\psi_{1}^{d-2g}A^{1}_{g,2g}.

We conclude that it is sufficient to prove that

(5.86)

\displaystyle B^{1}_{g,2g}=\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{g}(1,-1).

As a preliminary step, in Section
5.2.1
, we derive a new tautological relation using a variation of the method from the paper
[
LP11
]
by Liu and Pandharipande, and then in Section
5.2.2
, we use it to prove Equation (
5.86
).

5.2.1. A new relation via the Liu–Pandharipande method

We use the same notation as in Section
5.1
, with the following addendum to the notation. Let

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 21.0pt\blacked@@}}{\hbox{\kern 30.92847pt\blacked@@}}}}\ignorespaces}}\hbox{\kern 20.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{g}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.32985pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.12845pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\coloneqq\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{g}(-1,1).

.

Proof.

The proof is by localization in the moduli space of stable relative maps to
(\mathbb P^{1},\infty)
, which we review in detail in the appendix.

We consider the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)
of stable relative maps to
(\mathbb P^{1},\infty)
with
n=1
and
\mu=1
. The source curves of these maps have two marked points, and we assume that the marked point corresponding to the only part of
\mu
is labeled by
1
. Consider the
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
\mathbb P^{1}
given by

t\cdot[x,y]:=[tx,y],\quad[x,y]\in\mathbb P^{1},\;t\in\mathbb C^{*},

and the induced
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)
. Denote by

\pi\colon U\longrightarrow{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1),\quad f\colon U\longrightarrow\mathbb P^{1},

the
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant universal curve and the universal map, respectively. Consider a lifting of the
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
\mathbb P^{1}
to the line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb P^{1}}(-1)\to\mathbb P^{1}
with fiber weights
-1
,
0
over the fixed points
0,\infty\in\mathbb P^{1}
, respectively. The sheaf

B:=R^{1}\pi_{*}f^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb P^{1}}(-1))\longrightarrow{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)

is a
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant vector bundle of rank
g
.

We consider the following
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant cohomology class on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)
:

I_{g}:=\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{*}([0])e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(B)\in H^{2(g+1)}_{\mathbb C^{*}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)),

where
\mathrm{ev}_{2}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)\to\mathbb P^{1}
is the evaluation map corresponding to the second marked point,
[0]\in H^{2}_{\mathbb C^{*}}(\mathbb P^{1})
is the
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant cohomology class dual to the point
0\in\mathbb P^{1}
, and by
e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(\cdot)
we denote the
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant Euler class of a
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant vector bundle. Denote by

\epsilon\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)\longrightarrow{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}

the forgetful map, which is
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant with respect to the trivial
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}
. Consider the pushforward

(5.93)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}(I_{g}\cap[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)]^{\mathrm{vir}})\in H^{\mathbb C^{*}}_{2g}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}),

where
[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)]^{\mathrm{vir}}
is the
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant virtual fundamental class of the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)
. Since
H^{\mathbb C^{*}}_{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2})=H_{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2})\otimes_{\mathbb C}\mathbb C[u]
, where
u
is the generator of the equivariant cohomology ring of a point, the class (
5.93
) is a polynomial in
u
with coefficients in the space
H_{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2})
. The class
I_{g}\cap[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)]^{\mathrm{vir}}
considered as an element of
H^{\mathbb C^{*}}_{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1))\otimes_{\mathbb C[u]}\mathbb C[u,u^{-1}]
can be computed using the localization formula (
A.4
), and then using formulas (
A.5
) and (
A.6
), one can get an explicit formula for the pushforward of this class to
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}
in terms of tautological classes. The fact that the coefficients of the negative powers of
u
in the resulting expression vanish gives relations in the homology of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}
. Let us compute these relations explicitly.

Consider the connected components of the
\mathbb C^{*}
-fixed point set
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)^{\mathbb C^{*}}
. Apart from the component
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
formed by the stable relative maps with the target equal to
\mathbb P^{1}
, we have components
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}
labeled by decorated bipartite graphs
\Gamma
(see the appendix for details). Note that the decorated bipartite graphs
\Gamma
such that
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
have one of the following two forms:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{g_{1},g_{2}}:=\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern \displaystyle g_{1},g_{2}\geq 0.

Since
\iota_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{g_{1},g_{2}}}^{*}(\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{*}([0]))=0
, we have

, where we refer a reader to the appendix for a definition of spaces

Using Equations (

) and (

), we obtain the following equality in

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}(I_{g}\cap[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1}(\mathbb P^{1},1)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=

\displaystyle\;\epsilon_{*}\iota_{0*}\left(\iota^{*}_{0}\left(\frac{I_{g}}{e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}})}\right)\cap[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}]\right)

\displaystyle+\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}\geq 0,\,g_{2}\geq 1\\

and

Consider the component
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}

where

\Lambda_{g}^{\vee}(u):=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{g}(-1)^{i}\lambda_{i}u^{g-i}.

Using Equation (

), we obtain

\displaystyle\iota^{*}_{0}\left(\frac{I_{g}}{e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}})}\right)=\frac{\Lambda^{\vee}_{g}(-u)\Lambda^{\vee}_{g}(u)}{u-\psi_{1}}=(-1)^{g}\frac{u^{2g-1}}{1-\frac{\psi_{1}}{u}},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}\iota_{0*}\left(\iota^{*}_{0}\left(\frac{I_{g}}{e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}})}\right)\cap[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,2}]\right)=(-1)^{g}\sumop\displaylimits_{i\geq 0}u^{2g-1-i}\,\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\ellipsed@{5.56422pt}{6.12498pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 20.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{g}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.32985pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,1}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.7876pt\raise 6.85562pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.49452pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\,\,\psi^{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{2\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.12845pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Now consider the component

of the fixed point set. We have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}\iota_{\Gamma_{g_{1},g_{2}}*}\left(\iota^{*}_{\Gamma_{g_{1},g_{2}}}\left(\frac{I_{g}}{e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{\Gamma_{g_{1},g_{2}}}^{\mathrm{vir}})}\right)\cap[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma_{g_{1},g_{2}}}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\right)

5.2.2. Proof of Equation (
5.86
)

Let us prove by induction that

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\blacked@@}}{\hbox{\kern 28.92847pt\blacked@@}}}}\ignorespaces}}\hbox{\kern 18.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.32985pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12845pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces=\sumop\displaylimits_{k=1}^{\ell}(-1)^{k+1}\mathrm{perm}^{*}_{1,1}\Gamma^{g,1}_{2g\mathop{|}k}+\hskip-4.26773pt\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1},g_{2}\geq 1\\
d_{1}+d_{2}=2g_{1}-1\\

Indeed, for

, this is exactly Equation (

) when

. For the induction step, we have to check that

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1},g_{2}\geq 1\\
d_{1}+d_{2}=2g_{1}-1\\
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}(-1)^{d_{2}}\gamma^{g_{1}}_{d_{1}\mathop{|}\ell}\diamond\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\blacked@@}}{\hbox{\kern 33.4146pt\blacked@@}}}}\ignorespaces}}\hbox{\kern 18.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.816pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.98497pt\raise 6.92557pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{d_{2}}\,\,\,\,}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91684pt=\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1},g_{2}\geq 1\\
d_{1}+d_{2}=2g-1\\
d_{1},d_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.69046pt\gamma^{g_{1}}_{d_{1}\mathop{|}\ell}\diamond\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\ellipsed@{7.80728pt}{6.12498pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 18.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.816pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.90334pt\raise 6.92557pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\,\,\psi^{d_{2}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces+\hskip-7.11317pt\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}+g_{3}=g\\
g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}\geq 1\\
d_{1}+d_{2}+d_{3}=2(g_{1}+g_{2})-2\\
d_{1},d_{2},d_{3}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\hskip-7.11317pt(-1)^{d_{3}+1}\gamma^{g_{1}}_{d_{1}\mathop{|}\ell}\diamond\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.816pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.90334pt\raise 6.92557pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\,\,\psi^{d_{2}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\diamond\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\blacked@@}}{\hbox{\kern 33.4146pt\blacked@@}}}}\ignorespaces}}\hbox{\kern 18.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g_{3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.816pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{1\,}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.98497pt\raise 6.92557pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{d_{3}}\,\,\,\,}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.6146pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

which follows from Equation (
5.3
).

Since
\gamma^{g}_{d\mathop{|}k}=0
if
k>g
, substituting
\ell=g
in Equation (
5.2.2
), we obtain

\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{g}(1,-1)=\mathrm{perm}^{*}_{1,1}B^{1}_{g,2g},

which, because of the obvious property
\mathrm{perm}^{*}_{1,1}\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{g}(1,-1)=\lambda_{g}\mathrm{DR}_{g}(1,-1)
, implies Equation (
5.86
).

5.3. Proof of Conjecture
3
for
\boldsymbol{n=1}

As is proved in
[
BHIS22
, Theorem 2.4]
, for
n=1
, Conjecture
3
follows from Conjecture
2
.

6. Proof of Theorem
2.3

Consider an arbitrary CohFT
c_{g,n}
. In
[
BPS12
]
, the authors proved that

\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}_{0}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{a}{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}=\left.\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}_{0}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{a}{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}\right|_{t^{\gamma}_{c}=\delta_{c,0}v^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}},\quad 1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq N,\,a,b\geq 0,

where
v^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}:=\eta^{\gamma\mu}\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}_{0}}{{\partial}t^{\mu}_{0}{\partial}t^{1\!\!1}_{0}}
. Consider formal variables
v^{1},\ldots,v^{N}
and the associated algebra of differential polynomials
\mathcal{A}_{v}
. Define

{}^{[0]}_{\alpha,a;\beta,b}:=\left.\frac{{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{F}_{0}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha}_{a}{\partial}t^{\beta}_{b}}\right|_{t^{\gamma}_{c}=\delta_{c,0}v^{\gamma}},\quad P^{[0];\alpha}_{\beta,b}:=\eta^{\alpha\mu}{}^{[0]}_{\mu,0;\beta,b},\quad{\overline{P}}^{[0]}_{\beta,b}:=(P^{[0];1}_{\beta,b},\ldots,P^{[0];N}_{\beta,b}).

We see that for any
m\geq 2
, we have

\frac{{\partial}^{m}\mathcal{F}_{0}}{{\partial}t^{\alpha_{1}}_{d_{1}}\cdots{\partial}t^{\alpha_{m}}_{d_{m}}}=\left.\left(D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}^{[0]}_{\alpha_{1},d_{1}}}\cdots D_{{\partial}_{x}{\overline{P}}^{[0]}_{\alpha_{m-2},d_{m-2}}}{}^{[0]}_{\alpha_{m-1},d_{m-1};\alpha_{m},d_{m}}\right)\right|_{v^{\gamma}_{k}=v^{\mathrm{top};\gamma}_{k}},

which by Theorem
4.6
implies that

\intop\nolimits_{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,m+n}}B^{m}_{0,(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})}c_{0,m+n}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m+n}v_{j})=0

for any
m\geq 2
,
n\geq 1
,
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g+m-1
, and
v_{j}\in V
. Using the nondegeneracy of the Poincaré pairing in cohomology, we see that it is sufficient to prove the following statement.

Proposition 6.1
.

Cohomology classes
\omega\in H^{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n})
of the form

(6.1)

\displaystyle\omega=c_{0,n}(\otimes_{j=1}^{n}v_{j}),\quad\text{where $c_{g,n}$ is an arbitrary CohFT and $v_{j}\in V$},

linearly span the cohomology space
H^{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n})
.

Proof.

For
I\subset\llbracket n\rrbracket
,
2\leq|I|\leq n-2
, denote by
\delta_{I}\in H^{2}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n})
the cohomology class that is Poincaré dual to the fundamental class of the closure in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n}
of the subvariety formed by curves with exactly one node and marked points
x_{i}
with
i\in I
on one bubble, and marked points
x_{j}
with
j\in\llbracket n\rrbracket\backslash I
on the other bubble. The cohomology algebra of the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n}
is generated by the classes
\delta_{I}
(see,
e.g.
,
[
Man99
]
). Using the tensor product of CohFTs, we see that it is sufficient to prove the following special case of the proposition.

Lemma 6.2
.

Any cohomology class
\delta_{I}\in H^{2}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n})
can be obtained as a linear combination of cohomology classes of the form (
6.1
).

Proof.

Consider the CohFT
c_{g,n}^{(0)}
defined as follows:

•

V:=\mathbb C^{2}
with standard basis
e_{1},e_{2}
,

•

e:=e_{1}+e_{2}
,

•

\eta_{\alpha\beta}:=\delta_{\alpha\beta}
,

•

c_{g,n}^{(0)}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}e_{\alpha_{i}}):=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\alpha_{1}=\ldots=\alpha_{n}$},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

For
t\in\mathbb C
, define

R(z):=\exp(r_{1}z),\quad\text{where }r_{1}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&t\\
t&0\end{pmatrix}.

Let us apply this
R
-matrix to the CohFT
c_{g,n}^{(0)}
and denote the resulting CohFT (with unit) by
c^{(t)}_{g,n}
(we follow the approach from
[
PPZ15
, Section 2]
). It is clear that
c^{(t)}_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}v_{i})
depends polynomially on
t
and, moreover,

\mathrm{Coef}_{t^{k}}c^{(t)}_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}v_{i})\in H^{2k}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}).

For any
0\leq k\leq 3g-3+n
, this coefficient can be obtained as a linear combination of the classes
c^{(t_{j})}_{g,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}v_{i})
,
1\leq j\leq 3g-2+n
, where
t_{1},\ldots,t_{3g-2+n}
are arbitrary pairwise distinct complex numbers. Therefore, it is sufficient to check that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\delta_{I}=\mathrm{Coef}_{t}c^{(t)}_{0,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}v_{i})\quad\text{for some vectors $v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V$}.

Let us prove that Equation (
6.2
) is true for

v_{i}=\begin{cases}e_{1}&\text{if $i\in I$},\\
e_{2}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Indeed, we compute

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Coef}_{t}c^{(t)}_{0,n}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}v_{i})=

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{I}\subset\llbracket n\rrbracket\\
2\leq|\widetilde{I}|\leq n-2\end{subarray}}\mathrm{gl}_{*}\left(c^{(0)}_{0,|\widetilde{I}|+1}(\otimes_{i\in\widetilde{I}}v_{i}\otimes e_{\mu})\delta^{\mu,3-\nu}\otimes c^{(0)}_{0,n-|\widetilde{I}|+1}(\otimes_{j\in\llbracket n\rrbracket\backslash\widetilde{I}}v_{j}\otimes e_{\nu})\right)

\displaystyle-\sumop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}c^{(0)}_{0,n}(v_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes r_{1}(v_{i})\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{n})

\displaystyle+\pi_{*}\left(\psi_{n+1}^{2}c^{(0)}_{0,n+1}(v_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{n}\otimes r_{1}(e))\right),

where
\mathrm{gl}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,|\widetilde{I}|+1}\otimes{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n-|\widetilde{I}|+1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n}
is the obvious gluing map and
\pi\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n+1}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,n}
forgets the last marked point. From the definition of the CohFT
c_{g,n}^{(0)}
, it is easy to see that the first summand on the right-hand side of Equation (
6.3
) is exactly the class
\delta_{I}
, while the second and the third summands vanish. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

This concludes the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

7. A reduction of the system of relations in the case
\boldsymbol{m\geq 2}

Recall that in Section
3
, we introduced the polynomial
P_{g,n,m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in R^{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+m})[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
and considered the following cohomology classes:

(7.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,\overline{d}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Coef}_{x_{1}^{d_{1}}\cdots x_{n}^{d_{n}}}P_{g,n,m}

\displaystyle=\sumop\displaylimits_{T\in\mathrm{SRT}^{(b,nd)}_{g,n,m;\circ}}(-1)^{|E(T)|}\mathrm{e}_{*}[T,\overline{d}]\in R^{\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+m});

see Equation (
3.2
) and Lemma
3.8
. By Theorem
3.4
, an equivalent way to state Conjecture
1
is to conjecture that

(7.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{g,\overline{d}}^{m}=0\quad\text{for any $g\geq 0$, $n\geq 1$, $m\geq 2$, $\overline{d}\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}^{n}$ satisfying $d_{1}+\dots+d_{n}\geq 2g+m-1$}.

One more equivalent reformulation reduces the number of relations one has to check.

Theorem 7.1
.

The system of relations (
7.2
), as a whole, is equivalent to the following one:

(7.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{g,\overline{d}}^{m}=0\quad\text{for any $g\geq 0$, $n\geq 1$, $m\geq 2$, $d_{i}\geq 1$ satisfying $d_{1}+\dots+d_{n}=2g+m-1$}.

Proof.

For a tree
T\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,m}
and
h\in\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)
, let us use the more detailed notation
I_{h,T}
instead of
I_{h}
.

We start with the following lemma.

Lemma 7.2
.

We have
\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\dots,d_{n},0)}=\pi^{*}\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\dots,d_{n})}
, where
\pi\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+1+m}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+m}
forgets the marked point number
n+1

(
and shifts the numbers of the last
m
marked points
)
.

Proof.

Up to a relabeling of the marked points or legs in the stable rooted trees, it is convenient to assume that the marked point that we forget under the projection
\pi
is labeled by
0
and that the labels of all other
n+m
points are preserved by
\pi
. With this new convention, we have to prove that
\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(0,d_{1},\dots,d_{n})}=\pi^{*}\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\dots,d_{n})}
or equivalently

P_{g,n+1,m}(0,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\pi^{*}P_{g,n,m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}).

We use the formula (
3.1
) for the polynomial
P_{g,n,m}
and consider the contribution of a pair
(T,p)
,
T\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,m}
,
p\colon\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T)\to\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}
. From the pullback formula for the
\psi
-classes, we have

(7.4)

\displaystyle\pi^{*}\xi_{T*}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T)}\hskip-2.84544pt\psi_{h}^{p(h)}\right)=\hskip-2.84544pt\sumop\displaylimits_{v\in V(T)}\hskip-2.84544pt\xi_{T_{v}*}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T_{v})}\hskip-2.84544pt\psi_{h}^{p_{v}(h)}\right)-\hskip-5.69046pt\sumop\displaylimits_{f\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T)}\hskip-2.84544pt\xi_{T_{f}*}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T_{f})}\hskip-2.84544pt\psi_{h}^{p_{f}(h)}\right).

Here
T_{v}
is the tree
T
with an extra leg
\sigma_{0}
attached to the vertex
v
. Naturally,
\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{v})=\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\sqcup\{\sigma_{0}\}
, and we define
p_{v}(\sigma_{0}):=0
, and
p_{v}(h):=p(h)
for any
h\in\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)
. The tree
T_{f}
is obtained as follows (see also (
7
)):

•

If
f
is a leg
\sigma_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq n
, then we attach to it a new vertex of genus
0
and attach to this new vertex the leg
\sigma_{i}
and an extra leg
\sigma_{0}
.

•

If
f
is a part of an edge, then we break this edge into two half-edges, insert a vertex of genus
0
between them, and attach an extra leg
\sigma_{0}
to this vertex.

We have a natural inclusion
\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\subset\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{f})
with
|\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{f})\backslash\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)|=2
. The two half-edges from
\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{f})\backslash\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)
are attached to the new vertex of genus
0
: one of them is
\sigma_{0}
, and we denote the other one by
\widetilde{f}
. By definition, the function
p_{f}
coincides with
p
on
\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\backslash\{f\}
,
p_{f}(f):=p(f)-1
, and
p_{f}(\sigma_{0})=p_{f}(\widetilde{f}):=0
.

\displaystyle\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern
