3.0pt\raise-3.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{0}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.96474pt\raise 0.72679pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.25131pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.25pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}_{\,\,\sigma_{0}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.25551pt\raise-19.40009pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 68.88078pt\raise-8.9861pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 84.88078pt\raise-8.9861pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{{{\hbox{\ellipsed@{5.88078pt}{6.47221pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.88078pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-3.47223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{h}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}{{{}{}{}{}{}}}\ignorespaces{}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.09514pt\raise-9.54091pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.70335pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{p(f)-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{{{{}{}{}{}{}}}}\ignorespaces{{{}{}{}{}{}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 15.88078pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.88078pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 50.38078pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 68.88078pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 84.88078pt\raise-20.3333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}

This way, we see that taking the sum over all pairs
(T,p)
contributing to
P_{g,n,m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
and using (
7.4
), we can list all pairs contributing to
P_{g,n+1,m}(0,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
, and moreover the signs are exactly the ones we have to use in the formula for
P_{g,n+1,m}(0,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.

So we just have to check the coefficients. In the case of the pairs
(T_{v},p_{v})
, the equality of the coefficients is obvious because
\left.x_{I_{h,T_{v}}}^{p_{v}(h)+1}\right|_{x_{0}=0}=x_{I_{h,T}}^{p(h)+1}
for any
h\in\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)=\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{v})\backslash\{\sigma_{0}\}
. In the case of the pairs
(T_{f},p_{f})
, we have

\left.x_{I_{h,T_{f}}}^{p_{f}(h)+1}\right|_{x_{0}=0}=x_{I_{h,T}}^{p(h)+1}
for any
h\in\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\backslash\{f\}\subset\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{f})
. Regarding the remaining half-edges, note that
x_{I_{\widetilde{f},T_{f}}}=\left.x_{I_{f,T_{f}}}\right|_{x_{0}=0}=x_{I_{f,T}}
, which implies that
x_{I_{f,T}}^{p(f)+1}=\left.x_{I_{f,T_{f}}}^{p_{f}(f)+1}x_{I_{\widetilde{f},T_{f}}}^{p_{f}(\widetilde{f})+1}\right|_{x_{0}=0}
. Thus, the coefficients match for this type of trees as well, and we conclude that
P_{g,n+1,m}(0,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\pi^{*}P_{g,n,m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume that the relations (
7.2
) hold for all triples
(g^{\prime},n^{\prime},m^{\prime})
with either
g^{\prime}<g
,
n^{\prime}\leq n
or
g^{\prime}\leq g
,
n^{\prime}<n
, for
m^{\prime}=m
and
m^{\prime}=2
. Then the difference

(7.15)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i-1},d_{i}+1,d_{i+1},\ldots,d_{n})}-\psi_{i}\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})}

is equal to zero for any
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\geq 0
such that
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{n}\geq 2g+m-1
.

Proof.

Using the formula (
7.1
), we see that the pairs
(T,p)
,
T\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,m}
,
p\colon\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\to\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}
, contributing to the difference
\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\dots,d_{i}+1,\dots,d_{n})}-\psi_{i}\widetilde{B}^{m}_{g,(d_{1},\dots,d_{n})}
satisfy the property
p(\sigma_{i})=0
; these graphs come from the first summand. Obviously,
\sigma_{i}
cannot be attached to the root vertex (otherwise, the coefficient of
x_{i}^{d_{i}+1}
is equal to zero). So
\sigma_{i}
is attached to the vertex that is the first descendant of some
f\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T)
.

We cut the edge that contains
f
and obtain two trees,
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, of genera
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
, respectively. The regular legs of
T_{1}
are
\sigma_{j}
,
j\notin I_{f,T}
, and
f
, the root vertex is the original root vertex, and the frozen legs are the frozen legs of
T
. The root vertex of
T_{2}
is the one where
\sigma_{i}
is attached, the regular legs are
\sigma_{j}
,
j\in I_{f,T}\backslash\{i\}
, and the frozen legs are
\sigma_{i}
and the half-edge
f^{\prime}
that formed an edge with
f
in
T
. So
T_{1}\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g_{1},n-|I_{f,T}|+1,m}
and
T_{2}\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g_{2},|I_{f,T}|-1,2}
. We have natural inclusions
\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{1})\subset\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)
and
\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T_{2})\subset\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)
, and we denote by
p^{(1)}
and
p^{(2)}
, respectively, the restrictions of the function
p
to these subsets.

Example 7.4
.

Consider, for instance, the following tree
T
, which is cut into
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
at the dashed place on the picture:

Here we assume
n=5
,
m=3
,
g=g_{1}+g_{2}
, where
g_{1}=g_{1,1}+g_{1,2}+g_{1,3}
and
g_{2}=g_{2,1}+g_{2,2}
,
i=1
. The tree
T
is cut into two trees at the edge
(f,f^{\prime})
,
I_{f,T}=\{1,4,5\}
, and we set
\{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},f\}:=H^{e}_{+}(T)
.

Note that if
I_{f,T}\neq\{i\}
, then the number of regular legs both in
T_{1}
and in
T_{2}
is less than
n
. If
I_{f,T}=\{i\}
, then the genus of
T_{1}
is less than
g
.

We can express the contribution of the pair
(T,p)
to the difference (
7.15
) as follows:

(7.16)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Coef}_{x_{1}^{d_{1}}\cdots x_{i}^{d_{i}+1}\cdots x_{n}^{d_{n}}}\mathrm{gl}_{*}

\displaystyle\left[(-1)\hskip-5.69046pt\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket\backslash I_{f,T}}\hskip-7.11317ptx_{i}^{-1}\cdot(-1)^{|E(T_{1})|}\xi_{T_{1}*}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T_{1})}\hskip-7.11317pt\psi_{h}^{p^{(1)}(h)}\right)\hskip 0.0pt\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T_{1})}\hskip-7.11317ptx_{I_{h,T}}^{p^{(1)}(h)+1}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\left.\otimes\hskip-7.11317pt\prodop\displaylimits_{j\in I_{f,T}\backslash\{i\}}\hskip-7.11317ptx_{j}^{-1}\cdot(-1)^{|E(T_{2})|}\xi_{T_{2}*}\left(\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T_{2})}\hskip-7.11317pt\psi_{h}^{p^{(2)}(h)}\right)\hskip 0.0pt\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in\widetilde{H}_{+}^{em}(T_{2})}\hskip-7.11317ptx_{I_{h,T}}^{p^{(2)}(h)+1}\right],

where
\mathrm{gl}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{1},(n-|I_{f,T}|+1)+m}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{2},(|I_{f,T}|-1)+2}\to{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n+m}
is the natural gluing map that glues the marked point corresponding to the regular leg
f
on the curves of the first space and the marked point corresponding to the frozen leg
f^{\prime}
on the curves of the second space into a node.

Summing the expressions (
7.16
) over all pair
(T,p)
,
T\in\mathrm{SRT}_{g,n,m}
,
T\colon\widetilde{H}^{em}_{+}(T)\to\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}
, such that
p(\sigma_{i})=0
and
\sigma_{i}
is not attached to the root of
T
, we obtain that the difference (
7.15
) is equal to

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Coef}_{x_{1}^{d_{1}}\cdots x_{i}^{d_{i}+1}\cdots x_{n}^{d_{n}}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1},g_{2}\geq 0\end{subarray}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\sqcup J=\llbracket n\rrbracket\\
i\in J\neq\{i\}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{gl}_{*}\Big[\underbrace{x_{J}P_{g_{1},|I|+1,m}(X_{I},x_{J})}_{A:=}\otimes\underbrace{P_{g_{2},|J|-1,2}(X_{J\backslash\{i\}})}_{B:=}\Big]

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Coef}_{x_{1}^{d_{1}}\cdots x_{i}^{d_{i}+1}\cdots x_{n}^{d_{n}}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1}+g_{2}=g\\
g_{1}\geq 0,\,g_{2}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mathrm{gl}_{*}\Big[\underbrace{x_{i}P_{g_{1},n,m}(x_{1},\ldots,\widehat{x_{i}},\ldots,x_{n},x_{i})}_{C:=}\otimes 1\Big],

where by
X_{I}
we denote the tuple of numbers
x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{|I|}}
,
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{|I|}\}=I
. By the assumptions of the lemma, we have

\deg A\leq 2g_{1}+m-2+1,\quad\deg B\leq 2g_{2}+2-2,\quad\deg C\leq 2g_{1}+m-2+1\leq 2g+m-1,

which implies that the difference (
7.15
) is equal to
0
when
\sumop\displaylimits d_{i}\geq 2g+m-1
.
∎

Now we can complete the proof of Theorem
7.1
. With Lemma
7.3
, we can first prove the equivalence of (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) for
m=2
by induction on the pairs
(g,n)
ignoring the condition
d_{i}\geq 1
in (
7.3
). The condition
d_{i}\geq 1
is then restored by Lemma
7.2
. Once it is done for
m=2
, we can do it for any
m\geq 2
, again, first using Lemma
7.3
and induction on the pairs
(g,n)
ignoring the condition
d_{i}\geq 1
in (
7.3
) and then applying Lemma
7.2
to restore this condition.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

Theorem
7.1
reduces the whole system of tautological relations from Conjecture
1
to a finite number of relations of fixed degree
2g+m-1
for each
g
and
m
. The total number of relations to check is equal to the number of partitions of
2g+m-1
.

In particular, the crucial case for the application to the polynomiality of the Dubrovin–Zhang hierarchies for arbitrary F-CohFTs (see Section
4.3.2
), the case
m=2
, is reduced to
|\{\lambda\vdash(2g+1)\}|
relations for each
g\geq 0
. One of these relations is proved for any
g\geq 0
in Section
5.1
.

Appendix. Localization in the moduli space of stable relative maps

For convenience of a reader, we briefly review here the localization formula for the moduli space of stable relative maps to
(\mathbb P^{1},\infty)
, following the papers
[
GV05
]
(which presents the formula in a much more general setting) and
[
Liu11
]
(containing details in the case of stable relative maps to
(\mathbb P^{1},\infty)
).

A.1. Stable relative maps

For
m\geq 1
, we denote by
\mathbb P^{1}(m)=\mathbb P^{1}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\mathbb P^{1}_{m}
a chain of
m
copies of
\mathbb P^{1}
. For
l=1,\ldots,m-1
, let
q_{l}
be the node at which
P^{1}_{l}
and
\mathbb P^{1}_{l+1}
intersect. Let
q_{0}\in\mathbb P^{1}_{1}
and
q_{m}\in\mathbb P^{1}_{m}
be smooth points. Identifying
q_{0}
with
\infty\in\mathbb P^{1}=\mathbb P^{1}_{0}
, we obtain a chain of
m+1
copies of
\mathbb P^{1}
that we denote by
\mathbb P^{1}[m]
. In the case
m=0
, we define
\mathbb P^{1}[0]:=\mathbb P^{1}=\mathbb P^{1}_{0}
and
q_{0}:=\infty
. The component
\mathbb P^{1}=\mathbb P^{1}_{0}
is called the
root
component, and the components
\mathbb P^{1}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb P^{1}_{m}
are called the
bubble
components.

Given
g\geq 0
,
d\geq 1
,
n\geq 0
, and a partition
\mu
of
d
of length
h=l(\mu)
, a
stable relative map
to
(\mathbb P^{1},\infty)
is the following data:

(A.1)

\displaystyle(f\colon C\longrightarrow\mathbb P^{1}[m];x_{1},\ldots,x_{h+n}),

where
C
is a connected complex algebraic curve of genus
g
, with at most nodal singularities,
f
is a morphism, and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{h+n}\in C
are smooth pairwise distinct marked points with the following properties:

a)

We have the degree condition over each
\mathbb P^{1}_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq m
.

b)

We have
f^{-1}(q_{m})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{h}\}
, and the map
f
is ramified at
x_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq h
, with multiplicity
\mu_{i}
.

c)

We have the predeformability condition over each node of
\mathbb P^{1}[m]
.

d)

The automorphism group of (
A.1
) is finite, where in the target
\mathbb P^{1}[m]
, we allow automorphisms fixing all of the points from
\mathbb P^{1}_{0}\cup\{q_{m}\}
.

The space of isomorphism classes of stable relative maps is denoted by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)
. This space is connected, and it is endowed with a virtual fundamental class

[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in H_{2\cdot\mathrm{vdim}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu),\mathbb C),\quad\mathrm{vdim}=2g-2+d+h.

Given
g\geq 0
,
d\geq 1
,
n\geq 0
, and partitions
\nu,\mu
of
d
of lengths
k=l(\nu)
and
h=l(\mu)
, a
stable relative map to rubber
\mathbb P^{1}
is the following data:

(A.2)

\displaystyle(f\colon C\longrightarrow\mathbb P^{1}(m);x_{1},\ldots,x_{h+n+k}),

where
C
is a connected complex algebraic curves of genus
g
with at most nodal singularities,
f
is a morphism, and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{h+n+k}\in C
are smooth pairwise distinct marked points with the following properties:

a)

We have the degree condition over each
\mathbb P^{1}_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq m
.

b)

We have
f^{-1}(q_{0})=\{x_{h+n+1},\ldots,x_{h+n+k}\}
, and the map
f
is ramified at
x_{h+n+i}
,
1\leq i\leq k
, with multiplicity
\nu_{i}
.

c)

We have
f^{-1}(q_{m})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{h}\}
, and the map
f
is ramified at
x_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq h
, with multiplicity
\mu_{i}
.

d)

We have the predeformability condition over each node of
\mathbb P^{1}(m)
.

e)

The automorphism group of (
A.2
) is finite, where in the target
\mathbb P^{1}(m)
, we allow automorphisms fixing the points
q_{0}
and
q_{m}
.

The space of isomorphism classes of stable relative maps to rubber
\mathbb P^{1}
is denoted by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\sim}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)
. This space is connected, and it is endowed with a virtual fundamental class

[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\sim}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in H_{2\cdot\mathrm{vdim}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\sim}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu),\mathbb C),\quad\mathrm{vdim}=2g-3+k+h.

We define
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\sim}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu):=\emptyset
if
|\mu|\neq|\nu|
or if
|\mu|=|\nu|=0
.

One can also consider stable relative maps to rubber
\mathbb P^{1}
where the source curve is not necessarily connected. The moduli space of such maps will be denoted by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\sim,\bullet}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)
. Note that the genus
g
can be negative here. This space is not necessarily connected. The connected components can be described as follows. For
r\geq 1
, consider a decomposition

\llbracket h+n+k\rrbracket=\bigsqcupop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{r}A_{i},\quad g=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{r}g_{i}+1-r,\quad g_{i}\geq 0.

We denote by
A
the set of pairs

A:=\{(g_{1},A_{1}),(g_{2},A_{2}),\ldots,(g_{r},A_{r})\}.

Denote by

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)_{A}

the subspace of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)
formed by stable relative maps to rubber
\mathbb P^{1}
, where the source curve has
r
connected components, and for each
1\leq j\leq r
, all of the points from
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in A_{j}}
belong to one connected component of genus
g_{j}
. If
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)_{A}\neq\emptyset
, then it is a connected component of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\sim,\bullet}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)
.

Assigning to a stable relative map to rubber
\mathbb P^{1}
the cotangent space at the point
q_{0}\in\mathbb P(m)
gives a line bundle over
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu)
whose first Chern class is denoted by
\widetilde{\psi}_{0}\in H^{2}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu,\mu))
.

A.2.
\boldsymbol{\mathbb C^{*}}
-fixed points

Consider the
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
\mathbb P^{1}
given by

t\cdot[x,y]:=[tx,y],\quad[x,y]\in\mathbb P^{1},\;t\in\mathbb C^{*},

and the induced
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)
. For the localization formula, we will need a description of the connected components of the
\mathbb C^{*}
-fixed point set
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)^{\mathbb C^{*}}
.

We assume
2g-2+h+n>0
.

Consider a stable relative map
(f\colon C\to\mathbb P^{1}[m];x_{1},\ldots,x_{h+n})
from
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)^{\mathbb C^{*}}
. Then we have the following:

•

f^{-1}(\mathbb P^{1}_{0}\backslash\{0,\infty\})
is a disjoint union of twice-punctured spheres
S_{1},\ldots,S_{k}
,
k\geq 1
, and
f|_{S_{i}}\colon S_{i}\to\mathbb P^{1}_{0}\backslash\{0,\infty\}
is an honest covering map, whose degree we denote by
d_{i}
.

•

f^{-1}(0)
is a disjoint union of connected nodal curves
C^{(0)}_{1},\ldots,C^{(0)}_{p}
and of some number of points.

Regarding the behavior of
f
over
\infty
, there are two cases.

Case
1
:
m=0
. Then we have
f^{-1}(\infty)=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{h}\}
,
p=1
,
f^{-1}(0)=C^{(0)}_{1}
,
k=h
, and after a renumbering of the spheres
\overline{S}_{1},\ldots,\overline{S}_{h}
, we have
d_{i}=\mu_{i}
. Denote the space of such maps by
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)^{\mathbb C^{*}}
; it is connected. Note that
x_{h+1},\ldots,x_{h+n}\in C^{(0)}_{1}
, and therefore the curve
C^{(0)}_{1}
equipped with the marked points
C^{(0)}_{1}\cap\overline{S}_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq h
, and
x_{h+1},\ldots,x_{h+n}
is a stable curve from
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,h+n}
. Conversely, given a stable curve from
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,h+n}
, let us attach
h
copies of
\mathbb P^{1}
at the first
h
marked points and construct a map from the resulting curve to
\mathbb P^{1}
by sending the original stable curve to
0
and mapping the
i^{\mathrm{th}}
copy of
\mathbb P^{1}
to the target
\mathbb P^{1}
by
[x,y]\mapsto[x^{\mu_{i}},y^{\mu_{i}}]
. This gives a surjective map

\iota_{0}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,h+n}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0},

for which we have

\iota_{0*}[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,h+n}]=\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{h}\mu_{i}\cdot[\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}]^{\mathrm{vir}}.

Case
2
:
m\geq 1
. Then
f^{-1}(\mathbb P^{1}(m))
is a disjoint union of connected nodal curves
C^{(\infty)}_{1},\ldots,C^{(\infty)}_{r}
. Let us assign to our stable relative map a
decorated bipartite graph

\Gamma
as follows:

•

The vertices
v\in V(\Gamma)
are labeled by
0
or
\infty
, which gives a decomposition
V(\Gamma)=V^{0}(\Gamma)\sqcup V^{\infty}(\Gamma)
. The vertices from
V^{0}(\Gamma)
correspond to the connected components of
f^{-1}(0)
, and the vertices from
V^{\infty}(\Gamma)
correspond to the curves
C^{(\infty)}_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq r
.

•

A vertex
v\in V^{0}(\Gamma)
is called
unstable
if it corresponds to a point in
f^{-1}(0)
. All other vertices from
V^{0}(\Gamma)
are called
stable
. The sets of stable and unstable vertices are denoted by
V^{0}_{\mathrm{st}}(\Gamma)
and
V^{0}_{\mathrm{unst}}(\Gamma)
, respectively;
V^{0}(\Gamma)=V^{0}_{\mathrm{st}}(\Gamma)\sqcup V^{0}_{\mathrm{unst}}(\Gamma)
.

•

Each vertex
v\in V(\Gamma)
is decorated with a number
g(v)\in\mathbb Z_{\geq 0}
that is equal to

–

the genus of the corresponding curve if
v
corresponds to a curve,

–

0
if
v
corresponds to a point.

•

The edges
e\in E(\Gamma)
correspond to the spheres
\overline{S}_{1},\ldots,\overline{S}_{k}
. The edge
e\in E(\Gamma)
corresponding to
\overline{S}_{i}
is decorated with
d_{e}:=d_{i}
. By definition, we assign the same number to both half-edges
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
forming the edge
e
; that is,
d_{h_{1}}=d_{h_{2}}:=d_{e}
.

•

The graph
\Gamma
carries
h+n
legs
L(\Gamma)
that correspond to the marked points on
C
.

•

We say that an unstable vertex
v\in V^{0}_{\mathrm{unst}}(\Gamma)
is of

–

first type
if
n(v)=1
(the set of such vertices is denoted by
V_{\mathrm{unst}}^{0,1}(\Gamma)
),

–

second type
if
n(v)=2
and
|L[v]|=1
(the set of such vertices is denoted by
V_{\mathrm{unst}}^{0,2}(\Gamma)
),

–

third type
if
n(v)=2
and
L[v]=\emptyset
(the set of such vertices is denoted by
V_{\mathrm{unst}}^{0,3}(\Gamma)
).

If
v
is an unstable vertex of first type or of second type, then it is incident to exactly one edge
e\in E(\Gamma)
. Set
d_{v}:=d_{e}
. If
v
is an unstable vertex of third type, then it is incident to exactly two edges
e,\widetilde{e}\in E(\Gamma)
. Set
d_{v}:=d_{e}
and
\widetilde{d}_{v}:=d_{\widetilde{e}}
.

•

The graph
\Gamma
is connected.

Denote by
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}
the subspace of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)^{\mathbb C^{*}}
formed by stable relative maps with a given decorated bipartite graph
\Gamma
. If
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
, then it is a connected component of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)^{\mathbb C^{*}}
. Introduce the following notation:

g_{\infty}(\Gamma):=\sumop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{\infty}(\Gamma)}(g(v)-1)+1,\quad n_{\infty}(\Gamma):=\sumop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{\infty}(\Gamma)}|L[v]|-h.

Consider the set of pairs

A(\Gamma):=\{(g(v),H[v])\}_{v\in V^{\infty}(\Gamma)},

and denote by
\nu(\Gamma)
the partition of
d=|\mu|
given by the numbers
d_{e}
,
e\in E(\Gamma)
. Set

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}:=\prodop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{0}_{\mathrm{st}}(\Gamma)}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g(v),n(v)}\times{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{\infty}(\Gamma),n_{\infty}(\Gamma)}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu(\Gamma),\mu)_{A(\Gamma)}.

We have

[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}]^{\mathrm{vir}}=\prodop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{0}_{\mathrm{st}}(\Gamma)}[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g(v),n(v)}]\times\left[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g_{\infty}(\Gamma),n_{\infty}(\Gamma)}^{\sim,\bullet}(\mathbb P^{1},\nu(\Gamma),\mu)_{A(\Gamma)}\right]^{\mathrm{vir}},

and for the natural surjective morphism

\iota_{\Gamma}\colon{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma},

we have

(A.3)

\displaystyle\iota_{\Gamma*}[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{\Gamma}]^{\mathrm{vir}}=|\Aut(\Gamma)|\prodop\displaylimits_{e\in E(\Gamma)}d_{e}\cdot[\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}]^{\mathrm{vir}}.

A.3. Localization formula

For a topological space
X
with a
\mathbb C^{*}
-action, we denote by
H_{*}^{\mathbb C^{*}}(X)
and
H^{*}_{\mathbb C^{*}}(X)
the
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant homology and cohomology groups of
X
with coefficients in
\mathbb C
, respectively. We denote by
u
the generator of the equivariant cohomology ring of a point:
H^{*}_{\mathbb C^{*}}(\mathrm{pt})=\mathbb C[u]
. The equivariant Euler class of a
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant complex vector bundle
V\to X
is denoted by
e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(V)
.

We now consider the
\mathbb C^{*}
-action on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)
given in the previous section. The moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)
is endowed with a
\mathbb C^{*}
-equivariant virtual fundamental class, which abusing notation we denote by
[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in H^{\mathbb C^{*}}_{2\cdot\mathrm{vdim}}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu))
. The virtual localization formula for the moduli space
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)
proved in
[
GV05
]
gives the following formula for this class considered as an element of
H^{\mathbb C^{*}}_{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu))\otimes_{\mathbb C[u]}\mathbb C[u,u^{-1}]
:

(A.4)

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=\frac{[\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}})}+\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{decorated}\\
\text{graphs $\Gamma$}\end{subarray}}\frac{[\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{vir}})}\in H^{\mathbb C^{*}}_{*}({\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}(\mathbb P^{1},\mu))\otimes_{\mathbb C[u]}\mathbb C[u,u^{-1}],

where
N_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}}
and
N_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{vir}}
are the virtual normal bundles to the subspaces
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
and
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\Gamma}
, respectively. The following formulas are very useful for applications of the localization formula:

(A.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\iota_{0}^{*}e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}})}=\Lambda_{g}^{\vee}(u)u^{-1}\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{h}\left(\frac{\mu_{i}^{\mu_{i}+1}}{\mu_{i}!}\frac{u^{1-\mu_{i}}}{u-\mu_{i}\psi_{i}}\right),

(A.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\iota_{\Gamma}^{*}e_{\mathbb C^{*}}(N_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{vir}})}=

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{-u-\widetilde{\psi}_{0}}\prodop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{0}_{\mathrm{st}}(\Gamma)}\left(\Lambda_{g(v)}^{\vee}(u)u^{-1}\prodop\displaylimits_{h\in H[v]\backslash L[v]}\frac{d_{h}^{d_{h}+1}}{d_{h}!}\frac{u^{1-d_{h}}}{u-d_{h}\psi_{h}}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{1}{-u}}\times\prodop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{0,1}_{\mathrm{unst}}(\Gamma)}\left(\frac{d_{v}^{d_{v}-1}}{d_{v}!}u^{1-d_{v}}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{0,2}_{\mathrm{unst}}(\Gamma)}\left(\frac{d_{v}^{d_{v}}}{d_{v}!}u^{-d_{v}}\right)\prodop\displaylimits_{v\in V^{0,3}_{\mathrm{unst}}(\Gamma)}\left(\frac{d_{v}^{d_{v}+1}\widetilde{d}_{v}^{\widetilde{d}_{v}+1}}{d_{v}!\widetilde{d}_{v}!}\frac{u^{-d_{v}-\widetilde{d}_{v}}}{d_{v}+\widetilde{d}_{v}}\right),

where

\Lambda_{g}^{\vee}(u):=\sumop\displaylimits_{i=0}^{g}(-1)^{i}\lambda_{i}u^{g-i}.
